People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m here because… I don’t know, my talking has gotten really messed up. Like I can’t keep my words in a straight line anymore. I’ll start trying to explain something simple, like an overnight shift at work, and then somehow I’m talking about soccer, and then paint colors, and then patient charts, and by the time I notice it, everyone’s looking at me like they’re lost.

It’s not even that I’m trying to change the subject. In my head it feels connected for a second, like one thing leads to the next, but when it comes out loud it’s like I skipped all the bridges between the thoughts. People ask me to slow down or repeat what I mean, and I try, but then I end up going somewhere else again. It’s embarrassing, honestly. I can see it happening while I’m talking sometimes, and I still can’t really pull it back.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know… I mean, not like I’m seeing people standing in the room that nobody else sees, or hearing a clear voice talking to me, if that’s what you mean. I don’t think that’s happening.

Sometimes I second-guess myself, though, because when I’m tired or stressed, I’ll hear a noise and I’ll be like, “Was that someone saying something?” but then it’s just the hallway or somebody moving around. Or I’ll think I caught something out of the corner of my eye, but when I look there’s nothing weird there. I don’t know if that counts. It doesn’t feel like… a whole thing that isn’t there. More like I’m already on edge and my brain grabs onto stuff.

So, I guess my answer is no, not really. Not in a way I’m sure about.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think it does, but the talking is the part people notice first. Like, when I’m trying to write something down, I’ll start with what I meant to say and then halfway through I’m suddenly adding details that don’t really belong there, or I can’t tell what order things should go in. It’s like the same problem as when I’m speaking.

At work, if I’m trying to explain an overnight shift or what happened with patient charts, I’ll begin in one place and then my mind jumps — soccer, colors on the walls, some random thing someone said — and in my head it feels like there was a connection, but when I say it or write it, it comes out all broken up. Then I look back and realize it doesn’t really make sense the way I thought it did.

Organizing tasks is kind of like that too. I can start doing one thing, then something reminds me of something else, and suddenly I’m off in another direction. But the worst is definitely when I’m trying to talk, because I can see people’s faces change, like they’re trying to follow me and they can’t. That’s what made me come in.

back, the heavy empty mood and loss of interest have been there for most of this whole period, even during times when the camera worries or scattered talking were quieter.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode (Criterion B)\n  manifestation: There was a stretch of a few weeks when I wasn’t in that deep depressed place, but I still believed the cameras and supervisors were monitoring me and I kept acting guarded at work.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I’ve needed coworkers to double-check my rounds and charting, and at home I’ve fallen behind on bills, It got bad enough that employee health sent me for an emergency evaluation after I admitted I was thinking about suicide.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 5 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about 1 year and 5 months, starting after my job switched from steady day shifts to rotating nights and overtime.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 1 year and 1 month\n  manifestation: The beliefs about being watched and the disorganized behavior have been happening for about 1 year and 1 month.\n- key: depressive_episode_duration\n  name: Depressive Episode Duration\n  description: 1 year and 2 months\n  manifestation: The depression and loss of interest have been with me for about 1 year and 2 months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 22 / orderly_or_psychiatric_aide / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S021\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because… I don’t know, my talking has gotten really messed up. Like I can’t keep my words in a straight line anymore. I’ll start trying to explain something simple, like an overnight shift at work, and then somehow I’m talking about soccer, and then paint colors, and then patient charts, and by the time I notice it, everyone’s looking at me like they’re lost.\n\nIt’s not even that I’m trying to change the subject. In my head it feels connected for a second, like one thing leads to the next, but when it comes out loud it’s like I skipped all the bridges between the thoughts. People ask me to slow down or repeat what I mean, and I try, but then I end up going somewhere else again. It’s embarrassing, honestly. I can see it happening while I’m talking sometimes, and I still can’t really pull it back.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear or see things that aren't there?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, not like I’m seeing people standing in the room that nobody else sees, or hearing a clear voice talking to me, if that’s what you mean. I don’t think that’s happening.\n\nSometimes I second-guess myself, though, because when I’m tired or stressed, I’ll hear a noise and I’ll be like, “Was that someone saying something?” but then it’s just the hallway or somebody moving around. Or I’ll think I caught something out of the corner of my eye, but when I look there’s nothing weird there. I don’t know if that counts. It doesn’t feel like… a whole thing that isn’t there. More like I’m already on edge and my brain grabs onto stuff.\n\nSo, I guess my answer is no, not really. Not in a way I’m sure about.\n\nDoctor: Is this difficulty with keeping your thoughts in order when speaking happening in other areas of your life, like in your writing or when you're trying to organize tasks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it does, but the talking is the part people notice first. Like, when I’m trying to write something down, I’ll start with what I meant to say and then halfway through I’m suddenly adding details that don’t really belong there, or I can’t tell what order things should go in. It’s like the same problem as when I’m speaking.\n\nAt work, if I’m trying to explain an overnight shift or what happened with patient charts, I’ll begin in one place and then my mind jumps — soccer, colors on the walls, some random thing someone said — and in my head it feels like there was a connection, but when I say it or write it, it comes out all broken up. Then I look back and realize it doesn’t really make sense the way I thought it did.\n\nOrganizing tasks is kind of like that too. I can start doing one thing, then something reminds me of something else, and suddenly I’m off in another direction. But the worst is definitely when I’m trying to talk, because I can see people’s faces change, like they’re trying to follow me and they can’t. That’s what made me come in.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you find that your mood changes frequently or unexpectedly?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. I don’t know if I’d say it changes a lot, like flipping back and forth all day. It’s more like… most days I wake up and I already feel heavy. Like before anything even happens, there’s this empty feeling sitting there, and I’m just carrying it around.

And then after work, after a shift, it can hit harder. I’ll get out to my car and just sit there, and sometimes I’m crying before I can even make myself drive home. It’s embarrassing to say because I’ll just be sitting in the parking lot trying to pull myself together, like I’m waiting for it to pass enough that I can actually turn the key and leave.

So I guess the mood doesn’t feel unexpected exactly. It’s just there most of the time, and sometimes it kind of caves in on me.

I don’t think that’s happening.\n\nSometimes I second-guess myself, though, because when I’m tired or stressed, I’ll hear a noise and I’ll be like, “Was that someone saying something?” but then it’s just the hallway or somebody moving around. That’s what made me come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your mood changes frequently or unexpectedly?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say it changes a lot, like flipping back and forth all day. I’ll get out to my car and just sit there, and sometimes I’m crying before I can even make myself drive home. It’s embarrassing to say because I’ll just be sitting in the parking lot trying to pull myself together, like I’m waiting for it to pass enough that I can actually turn the key and leave.\n\nSo I guess the mood doesn’t feel unexpected exactly. I’m not sure. I don’t think I’ve had, like, several days where I was unusually happy or full of energy in a way that felt clearly different. I mean, I’ve had moments where I’m more wound up or more irritated, especially if I’m stressed or haven’t slept well, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing as what you’re asking.

Sometimes I can get kind of restless or snappy, but it doesn’t feel like some big high mood or anything. It feels more like I’m tense, or overwhelmed, or I can’t settle.

So… I guess no, not that I can point to. But I’m not totally sure what counts, because I don’t always notice things clearly when I’m in the middle of them.

additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode (Criterion B)\n  manifestation: There was a stretch of a few weeks when I wasn’t in that deep depressed place, but I still believed the cameras and supervisors were monitoring me and I kept acting guarded at work.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I’ve needed coworkers to double-check my rounds and charting, and at home I’ve fallen behind on bills, ignored friends, and let my apartment get out of control. It got bad enough that employee health sent me for an emergency evaluation after I admitted I was thinking about suicide.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 5 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about 1 year and 5 months, starting after my job switched from steady day shifts to rotating nights and overtime.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 1 year and 1 month\n  manifestation: The beliefs about being watched and the disorganized behavior have been happening for about 1 year and 1 month.\n- key: depressive_episode_duration\n  name: Depressive Episode Duration\n  description: 1 year and 2 months\n  manifestation: The depression and loss of interest have been with me for about 1 year and 2 months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 22 / orderly_or_psychiatric_aide / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S021\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Is this difficulty with keeping your thoughts in order when speaking happening in other areas of your life, like in your writing or when you're trying to organize tasks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it does, but the talking is the part people notice first. Like, when I’m trying to write something down, I’ll start with what I meant to say and then halfway through I’m suddenly adding details that don’t really belong there, or I can’t tell what order things should go in. It’s like the same problem as when I’m speaking.\n\nAt work, if I’m trying to explain an overnight shift or what happened with patient charts, I’ll begin in one place and then my mind jumps — soccer, colors on the walls, some random thing someone said — and in my head it feels like there was a connection, but when I say it or write it, it comes out all broken up. Then I look back and realize it doesn’t really make sense the way I thought it did.\n\nOrganizing tasks is kind of like that too. I can start doing one thing, then something reminds me of something else, and suddenly I’m off in another direction. But the worst is definitely when I’m trying to talk, because I can see people’s faces change, like they’re trying to follow me and they can’t. That’s what made me come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your mood changes frequently or unexpectedly?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say it changes a lot, like flipping back and forth all day. It’s more like… most days I wake up and I already feel heavy. Like before anything even happens, there’s this empty feeling sitting there, and I’m just carrying it around.\n\nAnd then after work, after a shift, it can hit harder. I’ll get out to my car and just sit there, and sometimes I’m crying before I can even make myself drive home. It’s embarrassing to say because I’ll just be sitting in the parking lot trying to pull myself together, like I’m waiting for it to pass enough that I can actually turn the key and leave.\n\nSo I guess the mood doesn’t feel unexpected exactly. It’s just there most of the time, and sometimes it kind of caves in on me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely energetic, irritable, or unusually happy for several days or longer?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t think I’ve had, like, several days where I was unusually happy or full of energy in a way that felt clearly different. I mean, I’ve had moments where I’m more wound up or more irritated, especially if I’m stressed or haven’t slept well, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing as what you’re asking.\n\nI wouldn’t say I suddenly become this really energetic person for days and days. If anything, I usually feel like I’m dragging myself through things. Sometimes I can get kind of restless or snappy, but it doesn’t feel like some big high mood or anything. It feels more like I’m tense, or overwhelmed, or I can’t settle.\n\nSo… I guess no, not that I can point to. But I’m not totally sure what counts, because I don’t always notice things clearly when I’m in the middle of them.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever felt abnormally happy or extremely energetic for more than a day?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. I don’t think so… not in the way you’re saying it, like abnormally happy or extremely energetic for more than a day. I’ve had times where I’ll feel a little more awake or kind of keyed up, but it usually doesn’t last, and it doesn’t feel like this big good mood or like I’m suddenly able to do everything.

Most of the time, if I’m energetic, it’s more like nervous energy, if that makes sense. Like I can’t sit still or I’m irritated, but not happy. I don’t remember having a stretch where people were like, “You’re acting unusually great,” or where I felt unstoppable or anything like that.

So… I’d say no, not really. Or at least not that I can clearly point to.

I’m not totally sure what to say about “changes,” because my sleep has felt kind of all over the place in a way that I don’t always track very well. Like, I don’t keep a record or anything, so when someone asks me directly, I start second-guessing myself.

manifestation: I used to sketch murals, cook for my friends, and play soccer on weekends, but now all of that feels pointless and I usually just ignore messages and stay in my apartment.\n- key: S026\n  name: Weight_Appetite_Change\n  description: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.\n  manifestation: I barely get hungry anymore, so a lot of days I just drink coffee at work and maybe eat vending-machine crackers, and my clothes have gotten loose.\n- key: S028\n  name: Psychomotor_Changes\n  description: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.\n  manifestation: People tell me I move like I’m underwater, and then other times I can’t sit still and keep pacing my apartment or rubbing my hands until they’re sore.\n- key: S031\n  name: Diminished_Thinking\n  description: Diminished ability to think or concentrate, or indecisiveness.\n  manifestation: I can read the same page of a patient note or a bill three times and still not know what it says, and even picking what to buy at the store can freeze me up.\n- key: S032\n  name: Death_Suicide\n  description: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.\n  manifestation: After bad nights I keep thinking it would be easier if I didn’t wake up, and sometimes the thought comes that I should just end it, even though I get scared and tell someone.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: When the depression is at its worst, I’m also most convinced the supervisors are watching me through the cameras, and my speech gets so tangled that I can’t explain myself clearly.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: depressive_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness (Criterion C)\n  manifestation: Looking back, the heavy empty mood and loss of interest have been there for most of this whole period, even during times when the camera worries or scattered talking were quieter.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode (Criterion B)\n  manifestation: There was a stretch of a few weeks when I wasn’t in that deep depressed place, but I still believed the cameras and supervisors were monitoring me and I kept acting guarded at work.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I’ve needed coworkers to double-check my rounds and charting, and at home I’ve fallen behind on bills, ignored friends, and let my apartment get out of control. It got bad enough that employee health sent me for an emergency evaluation after I admitted I was thinking about suicide.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 5 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about 1 year and 5 months, starting after my job switched from steady day shifts to rotating nights and overtime.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 1 year and 1 month\n  manifestation: The beliefs about being watched and the disorganized behavior have been happening for about 1 year and 1 month.\n- key: depressive_episode_duration\n  name: Depressive Episode Duration\n  description: 1 year and 2 months\n  manifestation: The depression and loss of interest have been with me for about 1 year and 2 months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 22 / orderly_or_psychiatric_aide / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S021\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely energetic, irritable, or unusually happy for several days or longer?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t think I’ve had, like, several days where I was unusually happy or full of energy in a way that felt clearly different. I mean, I’ve had moments where I’m more wound up or more irritated, especially if I’m stressed or haven’t slept well, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing as what you’re asking.\n\nI wouldn’t say I suddenly become this really energetic person for days and days. If anything, I usually feel like I’m dragging myself through things. Sometimes I can get kind of restless or snappy, but it doesn’t feel like some big high mood or anything. But I’m not totally sure what counts, because I don’t always notice things clearly when I’m in the middle of them.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt abnormally happy or extremely energetic for more than a day?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re saying it, like abnormally happy or extremely energetic for more than a day. I’ve had times where I’ll feel a little more awake or kind of keyed up, but it usually doesn’t last, and it doesn’t feel like this big good mood or like I’m suddenly able to do everything.\n\nMost of the time, if I’m energetic, it’s more like nervous energy, if that makes sense. Like I can’t sit still or I’m irritated, but not happy. I don’t remember having a stretch where people were like, “You’re acting unusually great,” or where I felt unstoppable or anything like that.\n\nSo… I’d say no, not really. Or at least not that I can clearly point to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep patterns lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure what to say about “changes,” because my sleep has felt kind of all over the place in a way that I don’t always track very well. Like, I don’t keep a record or anything, so when someone asks me directly, I start second-guessing myself.\n\nSome nights I feel like I’m lying there for a long time, just not really settled, and then other times I think I sleep but I still wake up feeling like I didn’t get much rest. My schedule with work can make it hard to tell what’s a pattern and what’s just me being tired from the day.\n\nSo… I guess I’ve noticed that it doesn’t feel very consistent, but I can’t say clearly, like, “this changed on this day” or anything like that.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you often feel anxious or worried about things in your daily life?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I mean, I do worry, but I’m not sure how much is “often” compared to what’s normal. Like, I’ll get caught up thinking about regular things—work, whether I said something wrong, whether I forgot something, money stuff, just… basic life things—and sometimes it feels like my mind keeps circling around it longer than it should.

But I don’t know if I’d describe it as constant anxiety exactly. Some days it’s more in the background, like I’m tense and checking myself, and other days I’m just tired and don’t even have the energy to worry properly, if that makes sense. I guess I do have a hard time letting things go once they get in my head, even if the thing isn’t that big.

So… yes, I worry about daily things, probably more than I should, but I’m not sure how to measure it.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t think so… not like that, anyway. I’ve had nights where I didn’t sleep much, or I was up late and then had to go to work or do stuff the next day, but I wouldn’t say I had a lot of energy from it. Usually if I don’t sleep, I feel kind of worn down or foggy, even if I’m still moving around.

I guess sometimes I can push through because I have to, like if there are responsibilities or I’m already tense, but that doesn’t feel like not needing sleep. It feels more like I’m running on stress, if that makes sense. Like my body is awake but I don’t feel good or refreshed or unusually energetic.

So… no, I can’t really point to a period where I was sleeping very little and still felt energized or like I didn’t need it.

It’s like the same problem as when I’m speaking.\n\nAt work, if I’m trying to explain an overnight shift or what happened with patient charts, I’ll begin in one place and then my mind jumps — soccer, colors on the walls, some random thing someone said — and in my head it feels like there was a connection, but when I say it or write it, it comes out all broken up. Then I look back and realize it doesn’t really make sense the way I thought it did.\n\nOrganizing tasks is kind of like that too. I can start doing one thing, then something reminds me of something else, and suddenly I’m off in another direction. But the worst is definitely when I’m trying to talk, because I can see people’s faces change, like they’re trying to follow me and they can’t. That’s what made me come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your mood changes frequently or unexpectedly?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say it changes a lot, like flipping back and forth all day. It’s more like… most days I wake up and I already feel heavy. Like before anything even happens, there’s this empty feeling sitting there, and I’m just carrying it around.\n\nAnd then after work, after a shift, it can hit harder. I’ll get out to my car and just sit there, and sometimes I’m crying before I can even make myself drive home. It’s embarrassing to say because I’ll just be sitting in the parking lot trying to pull myself together, like I’m waiting for it to pass enough that I can actually turn the key and leave.\n\nSo I guess the mood doesn’t feel unexpected exactly. It’s just there most of the time, and sometimes it kind of caves in on me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely energetic, irritable, or unusually happy for several days or longer?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t think I’ve had, like, several days where I was unusually happy or full of energy in a way that felt clearly different. I mean, I’ve had moments where I’m more wound up or more irritated, especially if I’m stressed or haven’t slept well, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing as what you’re asking.\n\nI wouldn’t say I suddenly become this really energetic person for days and days. If anything, I usually feel like I’m dragging myself through things. Sometimes I can get kind of restless or snappy, but it doesn’t feel like some big high mood or anything. It feels more like I’m tense, or overwhelmed, or I can’t settle.\n\nSo… I guess no, not that I can point to. But I’m not totally sure what counts, because I don’t always notice things clearly when I’m in the middle of them.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt abnormally happy or extremely energetic for more than a day?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re saying it, like abnormally happy or extremely energetic for more than a day. I’ve had times where I’ll feel a little more awake or kind of keyed up, but it usually doesn’t last, and it doesn’t feel like this big good mood or like I’m suddenly able to do everything.\n\nMost of the time, if I’m energetic, it’s more like nervous energy, if that makes sense. Like I can’t sit still or I’m irritated, but not happy. I don’t remember having a stretch where people were like, “You’re acting unusually great,” or where I felt unstoppable or anything like that.\n\nSo… I’d say no, not really. Or at least not that I can clearly point to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep patterns lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure what to say about “changes,” because my sleep has felt kind of all over the place in a way that I don’t always track very well. Like, I don’t keep a record or anything, so when someone asks me directly, I start second-guessing myself.\n\nSome nights I feel like I’m lying there for a long time, just not really settled, and then other times I think I sleep but I still wake up feeling like I didn’t get much rest. But I don’t know if that’s new exactly, or if I’m only noticing it more because you’re asking. My schedule with work can make it hard to tell what’s a pattern and what’s just me being tired from the day.\n\nSo… I guess I’ve noticed that it doesn’t feel very consistent, but I can’t say clearly, like, “this changed on this day” or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried about things in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I do worry, but I’m not sure how much is “often” compared to what’s normal. Like, I’ll get caught up thinking about regular things—work, whether I said something wrong, whether I forgot something, money stuff, just… basic life things—and sometimes it feels like my mind keeps circling around it longer than it should.\n\nBut I don’t know if I’d describe it as constant anxiety exactly. Some days it’s more in the background, like I’m tense and checking myself, and other days I’m just tired and don’t even have the energy to worry properly, if that makes sense. I guess I do have a hard time letting things go once they get in my head, even if the thing isn’t that big.\n\nSo… yes, I worry about daily things, probably more than I should, but I’m not sure how to measure it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt like you didn't need much sleep and still had a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like that, anyway. I’ve had nights where I didn’t sleep much, or I was up late and then had to go to work or do stuff the next day, but I wouldn’t say I had a lot of energy from it. Usually if I don’t sleep, I feel kind of worn down or foggy, even if I’m still moving around.\n\nI guess sometimes I can push through because I have to, like if there are responsibilities or I’m already tense, but that doesn’t feel like not needing sleep. It feels more like I’m running on stress, if that makes sense. Like my body is awake but I don’t feel good or refreshed or unusually energetic.\n\nSo… no, I can’t really point to a period where I was sleeping very little and still felt energized or like I didn’t need it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

Yeah… yeah, definitely. That’s actually been one of the things I notice the most, even if I don’t always talk about it because it sounds kind of dramatic when I say it out loud.

I used to have stuff I cared about, like I used to sketch a lot—murals, mostly, or ideas for murals. I’d get really into the colors and the shapes and where something would go on a wall, even if it was just in a notebook and not a real project yet. And I used to like cooking for my friends, too. Not anything fancy all the time, but I liked making food and having people over, feeling like I was doing something good for them. And on weekends I’d play soccer sometimes, which used to help me feel like I was still part of things.

Now it’s like… I can remember that I liked those things, but I don’t feel the pull to do them. It all just feels pointless, or like too much effort for no reason. People message me and I’ll see it, and I know I should answer, and then I just don’t. I’ll leave it there and stay in my apartment instead. It’s not even always that I’m choosing to be alone in some big way—it’s more like everything feels flat, and I can’t make myself care enough to start.

I don’t remember having a stretch where people were like, “You’re acting unusually great,” or where I felt unstoppable or anything like that.\n\nSo… I’d say no, not really. Like, I don’t keep a record or anything, so when someone asks me directly, I start second-guessing myself.\n\nSome nights I feel like I’m lying there for a long time, just not really settled, and then other times I think I sleep but I still wake up feeling like I didn’t get much rest. But I don’t know if that’s new exactly, or if I’m only noticing it more because you’re asking. My schedule with work can make it hard to tell what’s a pattern and what’s just me being tired from the day.\n\nSo… I guess I’ve noticed that it doesn’t feel very consistent, but I can’t say clearly, like, “this changed on this day” or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried about things in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I do worry, but I’m not sure how much is “often” compared to what’s normal. Like, I’ll get caught up thinking about regular things—work, whether I said something wrong, whether I forgot something, money stuff, just… basic life things—and sometimes it feels like my mind keeps circling around it longer than it should.\n\nBut I don’t know if I’d describe it as constant anxiety exactly. Some days it’s more in the background, like I’m tense and checking myself, and other days I’m just tired and don’t even have the energy to worry properly, if that makes sense. I guess I do have a hard time letting things go once they get in my head, even if the thing isn’t that big.\n\nSo… yes, I worry about daily things, probably more than I should, but I’m not sure how to measure it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt like you didn't need much sleep and still had a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like that, anyway. I’ve had nights where I didn’t sleep much, or I was up late and then had to go to work or do stuff the next day, but I wouldn’t say I had a lot of energy from it. Usually if I don’t sleep, I feel kind of worn down or foggy, even if I’m still moving around.\n\nI guess sometimes I can push through because I have to, like if there are responsibilities or I’m already tense, but that doesn’t feel like not needing sleep. It feels more like I’m running on stress, if that makes sense. Like my body is awake but I don’t feel good or refreshed or unusually energetic.\n\nSo… no, I can’t really point to a period where I was sleeping very little and still felt energized or like I didn’t need it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in interest or pleasure in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, definitely. That’s actually been one of the things I notice the most, even if I don’t always talk about it because it sounds kind of dramatic when I say it out loud.\n\nI used to have stuff I cared about, like I used to sketch a lot—murals, mostly, or ideas for murals. I’d get really into the colors and the shapes and where something would go on a wall, even if it was just in a notebook and not a real project yet. And I used to like cooking for my friends, too. Not anything fancy all the time, but I liked making food and having people over, feeling like I was doing something good for them. And on weekends I’d play soccer sometimes, which used to help me feel like I was still part of things.\n\nNow it’s like… I can remember that I liked those things, but I don’t feel the pull to do them. It all just feels pointless, or like too much effort for no reason. People message me and I’ll see it, and I know I should answer, and then I just don’t. I’ll leave it there and stay in my apartment instead. It’s not even always that I’m choosing to be alone in some big way—it’s more like everything feels flat, and I can’t make myself care enough to start.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It’s not even always that I’m choosing to be alone in some big way—it’s more like everything feels flat, and I can’t make myself care enough to start.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever felt like your thoughts were being controlled or influenced by something outside of yourself?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 22 / orderly_or_psychiatric_aide / high_school\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I’m sure the supervisors changed my shifts and watch me through the unit cameras to see if I’m unsafe, even when people tell me it’s just staffing shortages.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I mean, not exactly like somebody is moving my thoughts around like a remote control, if that’s what you mean. But I do feel like things are being… influenced, or like decisions around me are being made because of what people think is going on with me, even when they act like that’s not what it is.

At work, especially. I’m pretty sure my supervisors have changed my shifts around because they’re watching me and they think I might be unsafe or unstable or something. They’ll say it’s just staffing shortages, or somebody called out, or “we’re adjusting coverage,” but it doesn’t feel random. It feels too pointed. Like I’ll get moved off certain times or put somewhere different, and then I notice the cameras on the unit and I can’t stop thinking they’re checking to see what I’m doing, whether I’m acting normal, whether I’m a risk.

And people tell me, “That’s just how scheduling is,” or “The cameras are there for everybody,” and I understand that’s the explanation they give. I can repeat it back. But it doesn’t feel true to me. It feels like there’s another reason underneath it, and I’m the reason. Like they’re not saying it directly because they don’t want me to know, but I can tell by how the shifts change and how the supervisors look at me or don’t look at me.

So… I don’t know if that counts as my thoughts being controlled. But I do feel like something outside of me—my bosses, the cameras, the way the unit is set up—is influencing what happens around me and watching me, even when everyone keeps saying it’s not like that.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because… I don’t know, my talking has gotten really messed up. Like I can’t keep my words in a straight line anymore. I’ll start trying to explain something simple, like an overnight shift at work, and then somehow I’m talking about soccer, and then paint colors, and then patient charts, and by the time I notice it, everyone’s looking at me like they’re lost.\n\nIt’s not even that I’m trying to change the subject. In my head it feels connected for a second, like one thing leads to the next, but when it comes out loud it’s like I skipped all the bridges between the thoughts. People ask me to slow down or repeat what I mean, and I try, but then I end up going somewhere else again. It’s embarrassing, honestly. I can see it happening while I’m talking sometimes, and I still can’t really pull it back.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear or see things that aren't there?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, not like I’m seeing people standing in the room that nobody else sees, or hearing a clear voice talking to me, if that’s what you mean. I don’t think that’s happening.\n\nSometimes I second-guess myself, though, because when I’m tired or stressed, I’ll hear a noise and I’ll be like, “Was that someone saying something?” but then it’s just the hallway or somebody moving around. Or I’ll think I caught something out of the corner of my eye, but when I look there’s nothing weird there. I don’t know if that counts. It doesn’t feel like… a whole thing that isn’t there. More like I’m already on edge and my brain grabs onto stuff.\n\nSo, I guess my answer is no, not really. Not in a way I’m sure about.\n\nDoctor: Is this difficulty with keeping your thoughts in order when speaking happening in other areas of your life, like in your writing or when you're trying to organize tasks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it does, but the talking is the part people notice first. It’s like the same problem as when I’m speaking.\n\nAt work, if I’m trying to explain an overnight shift or what happened with patient charts, I’ll begin in one place and then my mind jumps — soccer, colors on the walls, some random thing someone said — and in my head it feels like there was a connection, but when I say it or write it, it comes out all broken up. Then I look back and realize it doesn’t really make sense the way I thought it did.\n\nOrganizing tasks is kind of like that too. I can start doing one thing, then something reminds me of something else, and suddenly I’m off in another direction. But the worst is definitely when I’m trying to talk, because I can see people’s faces change, like they’re trying to follow me and they can’t. That’s what made me come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your mood changes frequently or unexpectedly?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say it changes a lot, like flipping back and forth all day. It’s more like… most days I wake up and I already feel heavy. Like before anything even happens, there’s this empty feeling sitting there, and I’m just carrying it around.\n\nAnd then after work, after a shift, it can hit harder. I’ll get out to my car and just sit there, and sometimes I’m crying before I can even make myself drive home. It’s embarrassing to say because I’ll just be sitting in the parking lot trying to pull myself together, like I’m waiting for it to pass enough that I can actually turn the key and leave.\n\nSo I guess the mood doesn’t feel unexpected exactly. It’s just there most of the time, and sometimes it kind of caves in on me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely energetic, irritable, or unusually happy for several days or longer?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t think I’ve had, like, several days where I was unusually happy or full of energy in a way that felt clearly different. I mean, I’ve had moments where I’m more wound up or more irritated, especially if I’m stressed or haven’t slept well, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing as what you’re asking.\n\nI wouldn’t say I suddenly become this really energetic person for days and days. If anything, I usually feel like I’m dragging myself through things. Sometimes I can get kind of restless or snappy, but it doesn’t feel like some big high mood or anything. It feels more like I’m tense, or overwhelmed, or I can’t settle.\n\nSo… I guess no, not that I can point to. But I’m not totally sure what counts, because I don’t always notice things clearly when I’m in the middle of them.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt abnormally happy or extremely energetic for more than a day?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re saying it, like abnormally happy or extremely energetic for more than a day. I’ve had times where I’ll feel a little more awake or kind of keyed up, but it usually doesn’t last, and it doesn’t feel like this big good mood or like I’m suddenly able to do everything.\n\nMost of the time, if I’m energetic, it’s more like nervous energy, if that makes sense. Like I can’t sit still or I’m irritated, but not happy. I don’t remember having a stretch where people were like, “You’re acting unusually great,” or where I felt unstoppable or anything like that.\n\nSo… I’d say no, not really. Or at least not that I can clearly point to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep patterns lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure what to say about “changes,” because my sleep has felt kind of all over the place in a way that I don’t always track very well. Like, I don’t keep a record or anything, so when someone asks me directly, I start second-guessing myself.\n\nSome nights I feel like I’m lying there for a long time, just not really settled, and then other times I think I sleep but I still wake up feeling like I didn’t get much rest. But I don’t know if that’s new exactly, or if I’m only noticing it more because you’re asking. My schedule with work can make it hard to tell what’s a pattern and what’s just me being tired from the day.\n\nSo… I guess I’ve noticed that it doesn’t feel very consistent, but I can’t say clearly, like, “this changed on this day” or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried about things in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I do worry, but I’m not sure how much is “often” compared to what’s normal. Like, I’ll get caught up thinking about regular things—work, whether I said something wrong, whether I forgot something, money stuff, just… basic life things—and sometimes it feels like my mind keeps circling around it longer than it should.\n\nBut I don’t know if I’d describe it as constant anxiety exactly. I guess I do have a hard time letting things go once they get in my head, even if the thing isn’t that big.\n\nSo… yes, I worry about daily things, probably more than I should, but I’m not sure how to measure it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt like you didn't need much sleep and still had a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like that, anyway. I’ve had nights where I didn’t sleep much, or I was up late and then had to go to work or do stuff the next day, but I wouldn’t say I had a lot of energy from it. Usually if I don’t sleep, I feel kind of worn down or foggy, even if I’m still moving around.\n\nI guess sometimes I can push through because I have to, like if there are responsibilities or I’m already tense, but that doesn’t feel like not needing sleep. It feels more like I’m running on stress, if that makes sense. description: Symptoms where speech or thought appears scattered and difficult to follow due to a lack of logical or consistent flow.\n  manifestation: What brings me in is I can’t keep my words in a straight line anymore, like I start talking about an overnight shift and end up jumping to soccer, paint colors, patient charts, and I can see everyone getting lost.\n- key: S022\n  name: Disorganized_Abnormal_Behavior\n  description: A state where behavior and motor control are severely disturbed, appearing as movements or reactions inappropriate to the situation.\n  manifestation: At work I’ve caught myself rocking in the hallway, copying a patient’s tapping, or standing there with my gloves half on like I’m stuck, when I know I’m supposed to be doing rounds.\n- key: S024\n  name: Depressed_Mood\n  description: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists.\n  manifestation: Most days I feel heavy and empty from the time I wake up, and after shifts I’ll sit in my car crying before I can even drive home.\n- key: S025\n  name: Loss_of_Interest\n  description: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.\n  manifestation: I used to sketch murals, cook for my friends, and play soccer on weekends, but now all of that feels pointless and I usually just ignore messages and stay in my apartment.\n- key: S026\n  name: Weight_Appetite_Change\n  description: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.\n  manifestation: I barely get hungry anymore, so a lot of days I just drink coffee at work and maybe eat vending-machine crackers, and my clothes have gotten loose.\n- key: S028\n  name: Psychomotor_Changes\n  description: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.\n  manifestation: People tell me I move like I’m underwater, and then other times I can’t sit still and keep pacing my apartment or rubbing my hands until they’re sore.\n- key: S031\n  name: Diminished_Thinking\n  description: Diminished ability to think or concentrate, or indecisiveness.\n  manifestation: I can read the same page of a patient note or a bill three times and still not know what it says, and even picking what to buy at the store can freeze me up.\n- key: S032\n  name: Death_Suicide\n  description: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.\n  manifestation: After bad nights I keep thinking it would be easier if I didn’t wake up, and sometimes the thought comes that I should just end it, even though I get scared and tell someone.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: When the depression is at its worst, I’m also most convinced the supervisors are watching me through the cameras, and my speech gets so tangled that I can’t explain myself clearly.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: depressive_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness (Criterion C)\n  manifestation: Looking back, the heavy empty mood and loss of interest have been there for most of this whole period, even during times when the camera worries or scattered talking were quieter.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode (Criterion B)\n  manifestation: There was a stretch of a few weeks when I wasn’t in that deep depressed place, but I still believed the cameras and supervisors were monitoring me and I kept acting guarded at work.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I’ve needed coworkers to double-check my rounds and charting, and at home I’ve fallen behind on bills, ignored friends, and let my apartment get out of control. It got bad enough that employee health sent me for an emergency evaluation after I admitted I was thinking about suicide.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 5 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about 1 year and 5 months, starting after my job switched from steady day shifts to rotating nights and overtime.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 1 year and 1 month\n  manifestation: The beliefs about being watched and the disorganized behavior have been happening for about 1 year and 1 month.\n- key: depressive_episode_duration\n  name: Depressive Episode Duration\n  description: 1 year and 2 months\n  manifestation: The depression and loss of interest have been with me for about 1 year and 2 months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 22 / orderly_or_psychiatric_aide / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S021\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt like you didn't need much sleep and still had a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like that, anyway. I’ve had nights where I didn’t sleep much, or I was up late and then had to go to work or do stuff the next day, but I wouldn’t say I had a lot of energy from it. Usually if I don’t sleep, I feel kind of worn down or foggy, even if I’m still moving around.\n\nI guess sometimes I can push through because I have to, like if there are responsibilities or I’m already tense, but that doesn’t feel like not needing sleep. It feels more like I’m running on stress, if that makes sense. Like my body is awake but I don’t feel good or refreshed or unusually energetic.\n\nSo… no, I can’t really point to a period where I was sleeping very little and still felt energized or like I didn’t need it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in interest or pleasure in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, definitely. That’s actually been one of the things I notice the most, even if I don’t always talk about it because it sounds kind of dramatic when I say it out loud.\n\nI used to have stuff I cared about, like I used to sketch a lot—murals, mostly, or ideas for murals. I’d get really into the colors and the shapes and where something would go on a wall, even if it was just in a notebook and not a real project yet. And I used to like cooking for my friends, too. Not anything fancy all the time, but I liked making food and having people over, feeling like I was doing something good for them. And on weekends I’d play soccer sometimes, which used to help me feel like I was still part of things.\n\nNow it’s like… I can remember that I liked those things, but I don’t feel the pull to do them. It all just feels pointless, or like too much effort for no reason. People message me and I’ll see it, and I know I should answer, and then I just don’t. I’ll leave it there and stay in my apartment instead. It’s not even always that I’m choosing to be alone in some big way—it’s more like everything feels flat, and I can’t make myself care enough to start.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts were being controlled or influenced by something outside of yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not exactly like somebody is moving my thoughts around like a remote control, if that’s what you mean. But I do feel like things are being… influenced, or like decisions around me are being made because of what people think is going on with me, even when they act like that’s not what it is.\n\nAt work, especially. I’m pretty sure my supervisors have changed my shifts around because they’re watching me and they think I might be unsafe or unstable or something. They’ll say it’s just staffing shortages, or somebody called out, or “we’re adjusting coverage,” but it doesn’t feel random. It feels too pointed. Like I’ll get moved off certain times or put somewhere different, and then I notice the cameras on the unit and I can’t stop thinking they’re checking to see what I’m doing, whether I’m acting normal, whether I’m a risk.\n\nAnd people tell me, “That’s just how scheduling is,” or “The cameras are there for everybody,” and I understand that’s the explanation they give. I can repeat it back. But it doesn’t feel true to me. It feels like there’s another reason underneath it, and I’m the reason. Like they’re not saying it directly because they don’t want me to know, but I can tell by how the shifts change and how the supervisors look at me or don’t look at me.\n\nSo… I don’t know if that counts as my thoughts being controlled. But I do feel like something outside of me—my bosses, the cameras, the way the unit is set up—is influencing what happens around me and watching me, even when everyone keeps saying it’s not like that.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever heard voices or sounds that other people couldn't hear when you were alone?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`.   },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "patient_turn_index: 12\n(If 1, this is your first reply — return your chief complaint.)\nReturn the JSON now."

I’m not really sure. I don’t think I’ve heard, like, clear voices when I’m alone—like someone talking in the room or calling my name when nobody’s there. At least not in a way I could point to and say, “Yes, that definitely happened.”

Sometimes I hear noises and I’ll get stuck on them, like something in the hallway or the building settling or a phone buzzing somewhere, and I’ll stop and listen because I’m trying to figure out if it’s real. But I don’t know if other people would hear it or not, because usually I’m by myself when I notice it, so there isn’t anyone there to ask. And then if I check and nothing’s there, I just kind of tell myself maybe it was pipes or neighbors or something.

So… voices, no, not really. Sounds, maybe little things here and there, but I don’t know if that’s what you mean.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because… I don’t know, my talking has gotten really messed up. Like I can’t keep my words in a straight line anymore. I’ll start trying to explain something simple, like an overnight shift at work, and then somehow I’m talking about soccer, and then paint colors, and then patient charts, and by the time I notice it, everyone’s looking at me like they’re lost.\n\nIt’s not even that I’m trying to change the subject. In my head it feels connected for a second, like one thing leads to the next, but when it comes out loud it’s like I skipped all the bridges between the thoughts. People ask me to slow down or repeat what I mean, and I try, but then I end up going somewhere else again. It’s embarrassing, honestly. I can see it happening while I’m talking sometimes, and I still can’t really pull it back.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear or see things that aren't there?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, not like I’m seeing people standing in the room that nobody else sees, or hearing a clear voice talking to me, if that’s what you mean. I don’t think that’s happening.\n\nSometimes I second-guess myself, though, because when I’m tired or stressed, I’ll hear a noise and I’ll be like, “Was that someone saying something?” but then it’s just the hallway or somebody moving around. Or I’ll think I caught something out of the corner of my eye, but when I look there’s nothing weird there. I don’t know if that counts. It doesn’t feel like… a whole thing that isn’t there. More like I’m already on edge and my brain grabs onto stuff.\n\nSo, I guess my answer is no, not really. Not in a way I’m sure about.\n\nDoctor: Is this difficulty with keeping your thoughts in order when speaking happening in other areas of your life, like in your writing or when you're trying to organize tasks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it does, but the talking is the part people notice first. Like, when I’m trying to write something down, I’ll start with what I meant to say and then halfway through I’m suddenly adding details that don’t really belong there, or I can’t tell what order things should go in. It’s like the same problem as when I’m speaking.\n\nAt work, if I’m trying to explain an overnight shift or what happened with patient charts, I’ll begin in one place and then my mind jumps — soccer, colors on the walls, some random thing someone said — and in my head it feels like there was a connection, but when I say it or write it, it comes out all broken up. Then I look back and realize it doesn’t really make sense the way I thought it did.\n\nOrganizing tasks is kind of like that too. I can start doing one thing, then something reminds me of something else, and suddenly I’m off in another direction. But the worst is definitely when I’m trying to talk, because I can see people’s faces change, like they’re trying to follow me and they can’t. That’s what made me come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your mood changes frequently or unexpectedly?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say it changes a lot, like flipping back and forth all day. It’s more like… most days I wake up and I already feel heavy. I’ll get out to my car and just sit there, and sometimes I’m crying before I can even make myself drive home. It’s embarrassing to say because I’ll just be sitting in the parking lot trying to pull myself together, like I’m waiting for it to pass enough that I can actually turn the key and leave.\n\nSo I guess the mood doesn’t feel unexpected exactly. It’s just there most of the time, and sometimes it kind of caves in on me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely energetic, irritable, or unusually happy for several days or longer?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t think I’ve had, like, several days where I was unusually happy or full of energy in a way that felt clearly different. If anything, I usually feel like I’m dragging myself through things. Sometimes I can get kind of restless or snappy, but it doesn’t feel like some big high mood or anything. It feels more like I’m tense, or overwhelmed, or I can’t settle.\n\nSo… I guess no, not that I can point to. But I’m not totally sure what counts, because I don’t always notice things clearly when I’m in the middle of them.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt abnormally happy or extremely energetic for more than a day?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re saying it, like abnormally happy or extremely energetic for more than a day. I’ve had times where I’ll feel a little more awake or kind of keyed up, but it usually doesn’t last, and it doesn’t feel like this big good mood or like I’m suddenly able to do everything.\n\nMost of the time, if I’m energetic, it’s more like nervous energy, if that makes sense. Like, I don’t keep a record or anything, so when someone asks me directly, I start second-guessing myself.\n\nSome nights I feel like I’m lying there for a long time, just not really settled, and then other times I think I sleep but I still wake up feeling like I didn’t get much rest. Like, I’ll get caught up thinking about regular things—work, whether I said something wrong, whether I forgot something, money stuff, just… basic life things—and sometimes it feels like my mind keeps circling around it longer than it should.\n\nBut I don’t know if I’d describe it as constant anxiety exactly. Some days it’s more in the background, like I’m tense and checking myself, and other days I’m just tired and don’t even have the energy to worry properly, if that makes sense. I guess I do have a hard time letting things go once they get in my head, even if the thing isn’t that big.\n\nSo… yes, I worry about daily things, probably more than I should, but I’m not sure how to measure it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt like you didn't need much sleep and still had a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like that, anyway. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know if I’d call it “extremely” angry for a long time. I mean, I get irritated, sure. Like if people keep asking me questions or if something feels like it’s being handled in a stupid way, I can feel myself getting tense and short. But I don’t think I’ve had days and days where I’m just raging or ready to explode the whole time.

It’s more like… I get worn down, and then little things bother me more than they probably should. I might snap in my tone or get quiet because I’m trying not to say something I’ll regret. But extended anger, like staying furious for hours or days, I don’t really think so. Not that I’ve noticed. It’s not usually anger that sticks around like that for me.
